# Alexander Zverev

This article is about the German tennis player. For his father, the Soviet tennis player, see [Alexander Zverev Sr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Zverev_Sr.) For other people, see [Alexander Zverev (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Zverev_\(disambiguation\)).

**Alexander** "**Sascha**" **Zverev**[^cite_note-5] (born 20 April 1997) is a German professional [tennis](29773.md) player. He has a career-high singles ranking of world No. 2 by the [ATP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_of_Tennis_Professionals), first achieved in June 2022.[^cite_note-6] Zverev has won 26 [ATP Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Tour) singles titles, including two [majors](197638.md) at the [2026 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) and [2026 US Open](84011000.md), a gold medal at the [2020 Tokyo Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_at_the_2020_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), seven [ATP 1000](30872553.md) and the [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_ATP_Finals_%E2%80%93_Singles) and [2021 ATP Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_ATP_Finals_%E2%80%93_Singles).

Zverev is a former junior world No. 1, and won a junior major singles title at the [2014 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Boys'_singles). He had an early breakthrough on the professional tour, becoming one of the youngest [ATP Challenger Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Challenger_Tour) title winners in history at the age of 17. As a teenager, Zverev won two ATP titles and upset then–world No. 3 [Roger Federer](262376.md) on grass. At 20 years old, he became the youngest player to debut in the top 20 since [Novak Djokovic](16100029.md). At the [Laver Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laver_Cup), Zverev played an instrumental role in Team Europe's early success, winning the clinching matches in [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Laver_Cup), [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Laver_Cup), and [2026](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Laver_Cup).&#x20;

After reaching his first major final at the [2020 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he became an Olympic gold medalist in 2021. He suffered an ankle injury at the [2022 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), which required an extended recovery period. He rebounded in the following years to re-enter the top 10, where he has since remained a permanent fixture and reached five more major finals. In 2026, Zverev enjoyed his most successful [season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Alexander_Zverev_tennis_season) winning the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) and [US Open](84011000.md).&#x20;

## Early life and background

Alexander Zverev[^cite_note-10] was born on 20 April 1997 in [Hamburg](13467.md), Germany, to [Russian](59510.md) parents Irina Zvereva and [Alexander Zverev Sr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Zverev_Sr.) His older brother, [Mischa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mischa_Zverev), born nearly a decade earlier, was also a professional tennis player. Both of his parents were professional tennis players for the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union). His father, who ranked as high as No. 175 in the world, became the top-ranked men's player nationally, while his mother was the fourth-highest-ranked women's player in the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union). They both moved from [Sochi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sochi) to the capital to train at the [CSKA Moscow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CSKA_Moscow) military-run tennis club. The Soviet government often restricted their players from competing outside the country, an impediment that limited how high either of Alexander's parents could rise in the world rankings. With the [collapse of the Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Soviet_Union) imminent, Irina went to Germany to compete at a tournament in 1990, with her husband accompanying as her coach. While in Germany, they were offered jobs as tennis instructors. After initially declining, they accepted an offer to work at the [Uhlenhorster Hockey Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uhlenhorster_HC) in Hamburg the following year and ended up settling in the country.[^cite_note-economist-11]

Zverev, known in his family as Sascha (the Russian-language diminutive for Alexander), started playing tennis at the age of three. Since he began playing tennis at a very young age, he has said, "One day, when I was, I think, one year and five months old, I just picked up a little racket and I was starting to push the ball all over our apartment, and since then, they took me out on the court. I enjoy it still, I enjoyed it back then."[^cite_note-itf-junior-interview-12] When he was five years old, he started to play tennis at least half an hour each day.[^cite_note-itf-junior-interview-12] He was extremely competitive as a child. His brother, Mischa, said, "He would not understand or accept that he was losing," when the two would play against each other. He would never want to leave the court unless he won the match. He also played hockey and football as a child but decided to focus only on tennis around the age of twelve after an early-round loss at a high-level international junior tournament in Florida.[^cite_note-economist-11]

When Alexander was young, his mother was his primary coach while his father was focused on coaching his brother. He has said, "I think I have pretty good technique, which my mum did at a young age, so credit to her for that. My backhand, in particular, is 100 percent down to my mum."[^cite_note-telegraph-family-interview-13] While his mother had a more relaxed teaching style, his father "had a very Soviet way of doing physical training sessions" that involved doing timed drills for fixed numbers of repetitions.[^cite_note-economist-11] Alexander's coaches aimed for him to have a riskier, aggressive playing style built around hitting the ball with pace and finishing points quickly. This was a big contrast from how he played around age twelve when his style focused on being an "unbelievable fighter" from the baseline in part because he was too slow to go to the net.[^cite_note-telegraph-family-interview-13] Initially, Alexander struggled to change his playing style. He "made a lot of errors" and lost to opponents who excelled at keeping points alive. However, his father stuck with this strategy, saying, "We must practice fast tennis, aggressive tennis. If you lose today it's no big deal. You must think about the future."[^cite_note-economist-11][^cite_note-telegraph-family-interview-13]

## Junior career

Zverev played his first junior match in January 2011 at the age of 13 at a grade 4 tournament in Poland. Near the beginning of 2012, Zverev won his first ITF title at the Fujairah Junior Championships, a low-level Grade 4 tournament in the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md). He would pick up a lower level Grade 5 title at the Oman International Junior 2 a few weeks later, which led him to begin competing in higher-level events shortly before his 15th birthday.[^cite_note-itf-junior-profile-14] He did not have much success at tournaments that were Grade 2 and above until the following year when he reached back-to-back doubles finals with [Spencer Papa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spencer_Papa) at the Grade A [Copa Gerdau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copa_Gerdau) and the Grade 1 USTA International Spring Championships.[^cite_note-itf-junior-profile-14][^cite_note-\:3-15][^cite_note-16]

Zverev's early-season success in doubles proved to be the precursor of a major improvement in singles as well. During the European clay court season, he won his first Grade 1 title over [Andrey Rublev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrey_Rublev) at the Open International Junior de Beaulieu-sur-Mer.[^cite_note-17] He followed up that performance with his first Grade A title at the [Trofeo Bonfiglio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trofeo_Bonfiglio) a month later, becoming the youngest boys' singles champion in the tournament's history.[^cite_note-18] He also finished runner-up at the [2013 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Boys'_singles) to [Cristian Garín](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cristian_Gar%C3%ADn).[^cite_note-19][^cite_note-20] Zverev had some grass court success as well, finishing runner-up to [Nick Kyrgios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Kyrgios) at the Junior International Roehampton.[^cite_note-21] However, he needed to retire at [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Boys'_singles) due to a shoulder injury.[^cite_note-22] Zverev came close to reaching another major boys' singles final at the [2013 Junior US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Boys'_singles), but was defeated by the eventual champion [Borna Ćorić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borna_%C4%86ori%C4%87) in the semifinals.[^cite_note-us-open-loss-to-coric-23] This success was enough for him to take over the No. 1 ranking in late October. Before the end of the season, Zverev also represented Germany in the Junior Fed Cup, leading them to a fourth-place finish.[^cite_note-itf-junior-profile-14] His last tournament of the year was the Grade A [Orange Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orange_Bowl_\(tennis\)), where he was defeated by [Stefan Kozlov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefan_Kozlov) in the semifinals.[^cite_note-24] As the top-ranked junior at the end of the season, he was named the [ITF Junior World Champion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ITF_World_Champion), becoming the youngest boys' champion since [Donald Young](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Young_\(tennis\)) in 2005.[^cite_note-25]

Zverev played just two tournaments in 2014, both in Australia in January.[^cite_note-itf-junior-profile-14] He won the singles events at both tournaments, the first of which came against Australian [Omar Jasika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omar_Jasika) at the Traralgon Junior International. At the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Boys'_singles), he was able to defeat Kozlov, who was seeded second, to finish his junior career with a first major title.[^cite_note-26]

Junior Grand Slam results – Singles:

Australian Open: **W** ([2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Boys'_singles))\
&#x20;French Open: F ([2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Boys'_singles))\
&#x20;Wimbledon: 3R ([2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Boys'_singles))\

## Professional career

### 2011–14: ATP semifinal at 17



Zverev at the [2014 Bavarian International Tennis Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_BMW_Open)

At the age of 14, Zverev entered qualifying at three different tournaments, including the [2011 Moselle Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Moselle_Open) on the ATP Tour, but lost all of his matches. He won his professional main draw debut against compatriot Christian Lichtenegger at a Futures event in Germany in August 2012. At the end of the year, he made his first professional final, finishing runner-up to Florian Reynet at an ITF \$10K event in Florida.[^cite_note-itf-profile-27] Zverev continued to focus on the juniors in 2013 and did not reach another pro-level final that year,[^cite_note-core-tennis-28] but he did make his main draw debut on the ATP Tour in July, losing to [Roberto Bautista Agut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Bautista_Agut) at his hometown tournament, the [International German Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_International_German_Open).[^cite_note-first-revenge-29] He also made his [ATP Challenger Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Challenger_Tour) debut, losing to [Máximo González](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%A1ximo_Gonz%C3%A1lez) at the [Meerbusch Challenger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Maserati_Challenger) in August.[^cite_note-itf-profile-27]

After winning the boys' singles title at the [2014 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Australian_Open), Zverev shifted his focus to his professional career, only playing in pro events the rest of the year.[^cite_note-itf-junior-profile-14][^cite_note-core-tennis-28] Initially, he struggled on the pro tour, failing to qualify for the main draw at his first five events of the season. He did not win a main draw match until he recorded a single victory at the [Heilbronner Neckarcup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Heilbronner_Neckarcup) Challenger, his tenth event of the year. One of his losses was a retirement against his brother Mischa.[^cite_note-itf-profile-27] Zverev made his first professional breakthrough in July when he won the [Braunschweig Challenger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Sparkassen_Open) for his first professional title, despite entering the tournament with just one career Challenger-level match win and no top 100 victories. Three of the players he defeated were in the top 100, including his first-round opponent No. 87 [Tobias Kamke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tobias_Kamke), his semifinal opponent No. 56 [Andrey Golubev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrey_Golubev), and his final opponent No. 89 [Paul-Henri Mathieu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul-Henri_Mathieu).[^cite_note-30]

At the age of 17 years and 2 months, he became the youngest player to win a Challenger title since [Bernard Tomic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernard_Tomic) in 2009 and the twelfth youngest in history.[^cite_note-first-challenger-title-31] Zverev followed up this title with a breakthrough at the ATP Tour level. He entered the [International German Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_International_German_Open) having never won an ATP match but managed to reach the semifinals. He recorded four match wins at the event, including his first career victory against [Robin Haase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robin_Haase) and his first top 20 victory over No. 16 [Mikhail Youzhny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikhail_Youzhny) before losing to No. 7 [David Ferrer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Ferrer). He became the first 17-year old to defeat a top 20 opponent since [Richard Gasquet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Gasquet) in 2004 and the first to make a semifinal since [Marin Čilić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marin_%C4%8Cili%C4%87) in 2006.[^cite_note-Hamburg-semifinal-32][^cite_note-33] Zverev had risen from No. 665 to No. 285 after his Challenger title, and his [ATP 500 Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_500_Series) semifinal appearance took him to No. 161 in the world. He finished the season ranked No. 136.[^cite_note-atp-rankings-34]

### 2015: ATP doubles final, top 100



Zverev at the [2015 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles)

At the beginning of the season, Zverev was still ranked too low to receive direct entry into the main draws of ATP Tour events, which forced him to continue playing Challenger events through July. He also needed to enter qualifying for ATP events throughout the year. Zverev did not qualify for either of the first two major singles events of the season.[^cite_note-itf-profile-27] He was able to qualify for his first Masters event at the [Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Miami_Open) and reached the second round.[^cite_note-35] During the clay-court season, Zverev partnered with his brother Mischa to reach his first career ATP final in doubles at the [Bavarian International Tennis Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_BMW_Open). The Zverev brothers finished runner-up to top-seeded [Alexander Peya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Peya) and [Bruno Soares](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruno_Soares).[^cite_note-36] Later that month, he won his second career Challenger title at the [Heilbronn Neckarcup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Heilbronner_Neckarcup_%E2%80%93_Singles) to crack the top 100 of the ATP rankings.[^cite_note-37]

With a higher ranking, Zverev was directly accepted into [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles). A week before the tournament, he took part in the [Boodles Challenge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Boodles_Challenge) exhibition and surprisingly upset world No. 1 [Novak Djokovic](16100029.md) in straight sets.[^cite_note-38] Zverev backed up this performance in his Grand Slam tournament main draw debut, defeating [Teymuraz Gabashvili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teymuraz_Gabashvili) in a tight match that lasted until 9–7 in the fifth and final set.[^cite_note-39] He lost in the next round to [Denis Kudla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denis_Kudla). Zverev opted to play on clay after Wimbledon and reached another ATP semifinal at the [Swedish Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Swedish_Open) to put him back in the top 100 after one week out.[^cite_note-40][^cite_note-atp-rankings-34] In August, Zverev returned to the United States and competed in two US Open Series events. He notably upset No. 14 [Kevin Anderson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Anderson_\(tennis\)) at the [Citi Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Citi_Open) en route to the quarterfinals.[^cite_note-41][^cite_note-42] Zverev's brief rankings drop in July meant that he needed to qualify for the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles). He succeeded, but lost his opening round match to compatriot [Philipp Kohlschreiber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philipp_Kohlschreiber) in five sets.[^cite_note-43] Zverev won just one more main draw match the rest of the season and ended the year ranked No. 83 in the world.[^cite_note-44][^cite_note-atp-rankings-34] As the youngest player in the top 100, he was named the [ATP Newcomer of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Newcomer_of_the_Year).[^cite_note-45]

### 2016: First ATP title, Federer upset, top 20



Zverev at the [2016 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_US_Open_\(tennis\))

Zverev's first ATP event of the year was the [2016 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), where he had a difficult draw against world No. 2 [Andy Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Murray) and was only able to win six games in his opening match.[^cite_note-46] He bounced back at the [Open Sud de France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Open_Sud_de_France), reaching the semifinals in the singles event and his second career final with his brother in the doubles event.[^cite_note-47] During the singles event, he upset No. 13 Marin Čilić, the highest-ranked player he had defeated in an official match at the time.[^cite_note-48] Zverev then produced a strong result at the [Indian Wells Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_BNP_Paribas_Open), where he defeated two top 30 players. In the fourth round, he nearly upset No. 5 [Rafael Nadal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Nadal). Zverev had a match point while he was serving for the final set, but missed a routine forehand volley and then proceeded to lose 14 of the remaining 15 points in the match. He commented on the match point that, "I missed probably the easiest shot I had the whole match."[^cite_note-49] Back in Europe, Zverev was able to recover as he reached his first ATP singles final at the [Open de Nice Côte d'Azur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Open_de_Nice_C%C3%B4te_d'Azur), finishing runner-up to No. 15 [Dominic Thiem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominic_Thiem), the top seed and defending champion.[^cite_note-50] Thiem also defeated him in the third round of the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles).[^cite_note-51]

> "I haven't really grasped it yet. That I would be standing here as a winner, I couldn't have imagined it yesterday... It's unbelievable to get a win against Roger, especially on grass."

— Zverev on his victory against Federer at the Halle Open.

[^cite_note-federer-upset-quote-part-1-52]

[^cite_note-federer-upset-quote-part-2-53]

Zverev's next breakthrough came during the grass court season at the [Halle Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Gerry_Weber_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), where he upset world No. 3 [Roger Federer](262376.md) in the semifinals, ending Federer's streak of ten consecutive appearances in the final while competing at the tournament.[^cite_note-federer-upset-quote-part-2-53] He was also the first teenager to defeat Federer since Murray nearly a decade earlier.[^cite_note-federer-upset-quote-part-1-52] Nonetheless, he finished runner-up at the event to veteran compatriot [Florian Mayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florian_Mayer).[^cite_note-54] After this final, he entered the top 30 for the first time and stayed ranked in the 20s for the rest of the season.[^cite_note-atp-rankings-34] Despite being seeded at a major tournament for the first time, Zverev could then only match his best major singles result at [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), again falling to Berdych.[^cite_note-55] During the US Open Series, he reached the semifinals of the [Washington Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Citi_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles),[^cite_note-56] but lost his opening round matches at both Masters events. He was then upset in the second round of the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) by [Dan Evans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Evans_\(tennis\)).[^cite_note-57]

After the US Open, Zverev returned to Europe and won his first career ATP title at the [St. Petersburg Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_St._Petersburg_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles). He recorded his first win over No. 9 Berdych in the semifinals, and No. 3 [Stan Wawrinka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stan_Wawrinka) in the final, coming back from 0–3 down in the third set.[^cite_note-first-atp-title-58] At his next event, he then defeated No. 10 Thiem in the first round of the [2016 China Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_China_Open_\(tennis\)) for the first time in four tries this year. In doing so, Zverev became the first teenager to record three consecutive victories against top ten opponents since [Boris Becker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris_Becker) in 1986.[^cite_note-rivalry-with-thiem-2016-59][^cite_note-60] Zverev's third round appearance at the [Shanghai Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Shanghai_Rolex_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles) then helped him rise to No. 20 in the ATP rankings, making him the youngest player to debut in the top 20 since Novak Djokovic in 2006.[^cite_note-61]

### 2017: Two Masters titles, world No. 3

During the 2017 season, Zverev greatly improved his results at the higher level tournaments on the ATP Tour except for the four major events. It was at this time Zverev, who had been using his nickname Sascha, stated in a postmatch interview with Pam Shriver, that he would no longer be referred to as Sascha. Zverev stated that only his close family can refer to him as Sascha, as he was born in Germany and identifies as a German, and does not want a Russian nickname to be used for his professional career. At the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Australian_Open), Zverev again pushed Rafael Nadal to the brink, but ultimately lost in five sets.[^cite_note-62] His next ATP tournament was the [Open Sud de France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Open_Sud_de_France), where he won both the singles and doubles events. He and his brother defeated [Fabrice Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fabrice_Martin) and [Daniel Nestor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Nestor) for Alexander's first doubles title.[^cite_note-63] The following month, he made his first Masters quarterfinal at the [Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), upsetting No. 3 Stan Wawrinka along the way.[^cite_note-64] Nick Kyrgios defeated him at both Masters events that month.[^cite_note-65]

After a slow start to the clay court season, Zverev won two more titles in May, the first of which came at home in Germany at the [Bavarian International Tennis Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_BMW_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles).[^cite_note-66] He then followed up a second Masters quarterfinal at the [Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Mutua_Madrid_Open) with his first Masters title at the [Rome Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Italian_Open_\(tennis\)). He defeated Novak Djokovic in the final to become the youngest Masters champion since Djokovic in 2007 and the first such champion born in the 1990s. With the title, he also entered the top 10 for the first time.[^cite_note-first-masters-title-67][^cite_note-68] Despite his triumph in Rome, he lost his opening round match at the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles).[^cite_note-69] During the grass court season, Zverev made another singles final and again faced Federer at the [Halle Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Gerry_Weber_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), but could not defeat him this time.[^cite_note-70] He and his brother also finished runner-up in the doubles event to [Łukasz Kubot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%81ukasz_Kubot) and [Marcelo Melo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcelo_Melo).[^cite_note-71] At [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he achieved his best result at a major event to date, ultimately losing in the fourth round to the previous year's runner-up [Milos Raonic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milos_Raonic) in a tight five-set match.[^cite_note-72]

Back on hard courts, Zverev won his last two titles of the season in August. He won the [Washington Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Open_\(tennis\)) as well as a second consecutive Masters title at the [Canadian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Rogers_Cup), only dropping a single set at each tournament in each of his opening matches.[^cite_note-73] Notably, he needed to save three match points in his first match in Canada against [Richard Gasquet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Gasquet), including a 49 shot rally.[^cite_note-74] He then defeated Roger Federer in the final to become the first player outside of the [Big Four](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Four_\(tennis\)) to win multiple Masters titles in the same season since [David Nalbandian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Nalbandian) in 2007.[^cite_note-75][^cite_note-76] Despite this success, he was upset in the second round of the [US Open](82729.md) by fellow Next Gen player [Borna Ćorić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borna_%C4%86ori%C4%87).[^cite_note-77] At the end of the season, Zverev qualified for both the inaugural [Next Generation Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Next_Generation_ATP_Finals) as one of the top seven 21-and-under players, and the [ATP Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_ATP_Finals) as one of the top eight players in the world. He opted to skip the former event to focus on the latter.[^cite_note-78] At the ATP Finals, Zverev was grouped with Roger Federer, Marin Čilić, and [Jack Sock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Sock). He defeated Čilić in his first match, but lost his final two matches and did not advance out of his round robin group.[^cite_note-79][^cite_note-80][^cite_note-81] Zverev finished the year ranked No. 4, peaking at No. 3 right before the ATP Finals, and accumulated five ATP titles from just six finals.[^cite_note-itf-profile-27][^cite_note-atp-rankings-34]

### 2018: ATP Finals champion



Zverev with the champion's trophy at the [2018 Washington Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Citi_Open)

Zverev maintained his top 5 ranking throughout the year.[^cite_note-atp-rankings-34] However, he still did not deliver any high-calibre results at the major tournaments. He was upset at the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Australian_Open) by reigning Next Gen Finals champion and No. 59 [Chung Hyeon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chung_Hyeon) in five sets in the third round. Zverev stated that issues at majors were "definitely not physical" when asked if his problems were physical or mental, and also attribute this lack of success to the extra pressure he was putting on himself at these events.[^cite_note-82] Zverev did not reach his first final of the year until early April at the [Miami Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Miami_Open). Despite taking the first set of the final, he finished runner-up to American [John Isner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Isner), who had never previously won a Masters title.[^cite_note-83]

Zverev continued his Masters success into the clay-court season, reaching the semifinals at the [Monte Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Monte-Carlo_Masters),[^cite_note-84] winning his third career Masters title at the [Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Mutua_Madrid_Open), and making the final at the [Rome Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Italian_Open_\(tennis\)). In Madrid, he defeated Dominic Thiem in the final to become the only active player outside of the Big Four to have won three Masters titles.[^cite_note-85] He came close to winning back-to-back Masters events, going up a break in the third set against Nadal at the Italian Open. However, Nadal was able to recover and win the final five games of the match after a rain delay.[^cite_note-86] Zverev had also defended his title at the [Bavarian International Tennis Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_BMW_Open) in his only other French Open tune-up to help build up a 13 match win streak that lasted until the Italian Open final.[^cite_note-87] He capped off his excellent clay court season by reaching his first major quarterfinal at the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_French_Open). He needed to win three five-set matches to get that far before Thiem ended his run while he was faced with a hamstring injury.[^cite_note-88]

Up until the year-end championships, Zverev struggled to build on his early season success. He lost in the third round at both [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Wimbledon_Championships) and the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_US_Open_\(tennis\)),[^cite_note-89][^cite_note-90] and his best result at the four remaining Masters events was a semifinal at the [Shanghai Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Shanghai_Rolex_Masters).[^cite_note-91] He was able to defend his title at the [Washington Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Citi_Open), his only title during this period.[^cite_note-92] Zverev also reached two more doubles finals with his brother, but did not win either of them.[^cite_note-93][^cite_note-94] At the end of the season, Zverev qualified for both the [Next Generation Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Next_Generation_ATP_Finals) and the [ATP Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_ATP_Finals) for the second consecutive year, again choosing to only compete at the latter event. He was placed in a group with Novak Djokovic, Marin Čilić, and John Isner. This year, Zverev was able to advance out of the group, only losing to No. 1 Djokovic in the round robin.[^cite_note-95][^cite_note-96] He faced Federer in the semifinals and defeated him in straight sets to set up a rematch with Djokovic.[^cite_note-97] Despite being a heavy underdog and having just lost to Djokovic earlier in the week, Zverev won the final in straight sets for the tenth and biggest title of his career. He became the youngest tour champion since Djokovic a decade earlier and the first German to win the season-ending championships since Boris Becker in 1995. This was also Zverev's first victory over a current world No. 1 player.[^cite_note-98][^cite_note-99]

### 2019: Sixth Masters final



Zverev at the [2019 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_French_Open)

Zverev opened his [2019 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_ATP_Tour) with a fourth round appearance at the [2019 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), defeating [Jérémy Chardy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%A9r%C3%A9my_Chardy) in five sets in the second round before losing to [Milos Raonic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milos_Raonic) in straight sets.[^cite_note-100][^cite_note-101] Zverev next played at the [Mexican Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Abierto_Mexicano_Telcel) and finished runner-up to [Nick Kyrgios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Kyrgios).[^cite_note-102] Following this tournament, he did not win more than one match at any of his next six events, a streak that ended with two match wins at the [Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Mutua_Madrid_Open).[^cite_note-itf-profile-27][^cite_note-103] Nonetheless, Zverev finished the clay court season strong. The week before the French Open, he won his only title of the year at the [Geneva Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Geneva_Open), defeating [Nicolás Jarry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicol%C3%A1s_Jarry) in the final after saving two match points in the third set tiebreak.[^cite_note-104] He then went on to defeat No. 12 [Fabio Fognini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fabio_Fognini) and reach his second consecutive quarterfinal at the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), where he lost to [Novak Djokovic](16100029.md).[^cite_note-105] Zverev did not follow through on that success into the grass court season, where his best result was a quarterfinal at the [Halle Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Halle_Open).[^cite_note-106] He was upset in the first round at both the [Stuttgart Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_MercedesCup) and [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), the latter of which to qualifier [Jiří Veselý](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ji%C5%99%C3%AD_Vesel%C3%BD).[^cite_note-107] Zverev attributed his early season struggles to being distracted by a legal dispute with his former agent [Patricio Apey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patricio_Apey), with whom he had split from in the offseason.[^cite_note-108]

Zverev began to turn his season around following Wimbledon. He reached the semifinals of the [German Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Hamburg_European_Open) and the quarterfinals at the [Canadian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Rogers_Cup).[^cite_note-109][^cite_note-110] He then reached the last 16 at the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) for the first time, where he lost to seed No. 20 [Diego Schwartzman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diego_Schwartzman) to continue his lack of success at the major tournaments.[^cite_note-111] He had also fallen out of the top 5 in early August for the first time in nearly two years.[^cite_note-atp-rankings-34] Zverev's best result of the season came at the [Shanghai Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Shanghai_Masters). He upset No. 3 [Roger Federer](262376.md) in the quarterfinals before finishing runner-up to No. 4 [Daniil Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniil_Medvedev),[^cite_note-112][^cite_note-113] who was playing in his sixth consecutive final.[^cite_note-114] This performance helped him qualify for the [ATP Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_ATP_Finals) at the end of the year. At the event, Zverev was drawn into a round-robin group with [Rafael Nadal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Nadal), [Stefanos Tsitsipas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefanos_Tsitsipas), and Daniil Medvedev. He defeated Nadal for the first time in his opening match before losing to Tsitsipas.[^cite_note-115] He then won his match against Medvedev, which he had needed to win to advance via the tiebreak criteria. Nonetheless, he could not defend his title, losing to [Dominic Thiem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominic_Thiem) in the semifinals.[^cite_note-116] Zverev finished the season at No. 7 in the world.[^cite_note-atp-rankings-34]

### 2020: US Open final

Zverev began his [2020 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_ATP_Tour) at the inaugural [ATP Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_ATP_Cup). Germany was in Group F alongside Australia, Greece, and Canada. Against Australia, he lost to [Alex de Minaur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_de_Minaur) in three sets.[^cite_note-117] Australia won the tie over Germany 3–0.[^cite_note-118] Against Greece, he was defeated by [Stefanos Tsitsipas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefanos_Tsitsipas).[^cite_note-119] Germany won the tie over Greece 2–1.[^cite_note-120] Against Canada, he was beaten by [Denis Shapovalov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denis_Shapovalov).[^cite_note-121][^cite_note-122] Canada won the tie over Germany 2–1.[^cite_note-123] Germany ended third in Group F. Seeded seventh at the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he first defeated [Marco Cecchinato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Cecchinato) in the first round in straight sets.[^cite_note-124] After the match, he pledged to donate all his prize money from the tournament to relief efforts for the [ongoing bushfires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%9320_Australian_bushfire_season) if he won the title, a total of A\$4.12 million.[^cite_note-125][^cite_note-126] He then defeated [Egor Gerasimov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egor_Gerasimov), [Fernando Verdasco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Verdasco), and 17th seed [Andrey Rublev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrey_Rublev) to reach the quarterfinals without dropping a set. He defeated 15th seed and 2014 champion, [Stan Wawrinka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stan_Wawrinka), in the quarterfinals in four sets to reach his first major singles semifinal.[^cite_note-127] He fell in his semifinal match to fifth seed, [Dominic Thiem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominic_Thiem), in four sets.[^cite_note-128]

During the week of 24 February, Zverev played at the [Mexican Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Abierto_Mexicano_Telcel_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles). Seeded second, he was upset in his second-round match by qualifier [Tommy Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tommy_Paul_\(tennis\)).[^cite_note-129] Starting from 12 March through early August, the ATP suspended tournament play due to the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md).[^cite_note-130][^cite_note-131][^cite_note-132] At the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Zverev was seeded fifth. He defeated [Kevin Anderson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Anderson_\(tennis\)) in four sets, then beat 19-year old Brandon Nakashima in four sets. In the third round, he beat [Adrian Mannarino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adrian_Mannarino) in four sets before beating [Alejandro Davidovich Fokina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alejandro_Davidovich_Fokina) in straight sets. In the quarterfinals, he beat [Borna Ćorić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borna_%C4%86ori%C4%87) in four sets to reach his second major semifinal. There, he lost the first two sets to [Pablo Carreño Busta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pablo_Carre%C3%B1o_Busta), but came back to win the match in five; this was his first-ever match win from two sets down. He thus advanced to his first major final, where he faced [Dominic Thiem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominic_Thiem). He became the runner-up, losing the final in a final-set tiebreaker despite leading by two sets and twice coming within two points of the title in the final set.[^cite_note-133]

Zverev was seeded 6th at the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles). He beat [Dennis Novak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dennis_Novak), [Pierre-Hugues Herbert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre-Hugues_Herbert), and [Marco Cecchinato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Cecchinato), before losing in four sets to [Jannik Sinner](60061043.md).[^cite_note-134] In October, he won two consecutive ATP 250 events in Cologne, which took place in the [2020 ATP Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_ATP_Tour) because of the cancellation of several tournaments due to the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md). In the finals, he defeated [Félix Auger-Aliassime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F%C3%A9lix_Auger-Aliassime) and [Diego Schwartzman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diego_Schwartzman), both in straight sets.[^cite_note-135] Zverev then reached the final of the [Paris Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Rolex_Paris_Masters), beating Nadal in straight sets in the semifinals,[^cite_note-136] where he lost to [Daniil Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniil_Medvedev) in three sets.[^cite_note-137] In the [ATP Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_ATP_Finals_%E2%80%93_Singles), Zverev was eliminated in the group stage after a three-set win over [Diego Schwartzman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diego_Schwartzman), and straight-set losses to [Djokovic](16100029.md) and the eventual champion [Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniil_Medvedev).[^cite_note-138]

### 2021: Olympic gold, second ATP Finals title

Zverev began his season in February with the [ATP Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_ATP_Cup), where he represented Germany with [Jan-Lennard Struff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan-Lennard_Struff), [Kevin Krawietz](23521626.md) and [Andreas Mies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andreas_Mies) to reach the semi-finals. In his first Grand Slam tournament appearance at the 2021 [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he defeated [Marcos Giron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcos_Giron), [Maxime Cressy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maxime_Cressy), [Adrian Mannarino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adrian_Mannarino) and [Dušan Lajović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Du%C5%A1an_Lajovi%C4%87). His run ended in the quarterfinals, where he lost to the eventual champion and world No. 1 Novak Djokovic. In March his first tournament was in [Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_ABN_AMRO_World_Tennis_Tournament_%E2%80%93_Singles), where he lost in the first round to Kazakh [Alexander Bublik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Bublik).[^cite_note-139] He continued his performances in [Acapulco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Abierto_Mexicano_Telcel_%E2%80%93_Singles), where he won his 14th ATP title by defeating Stefanos Tsitsipas in the final.[^cite_note-140] At the tournament in [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he lost in the second round to Finland's [Emil Ruusuvuori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emil_Ruusuvuori).[^cite_note-141]

At his first tournament in 2021 on clay in [Monte Carlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Monte-Carlo_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles), Zverev finished in the third round, losing to [David Goffin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Goffin).[^cite_note-142] In Munich, he reached the quarterfinals, losing to the 107th ranked tennis player in the world, [Ilya Ivashka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilya_Ivashka).[^cite_note-143] At the Madrid Open, Zverev defeated Rafael Nadal, Dominic Thiem, and [Matteo Berrettini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matteo_Berrettini) en route to his fourth Masters 1000 title and 15th career title.[^cite_note-144] At the same tournament, he also reached his first semifinal in doubles at a Masters 1000, partnering with compatriot [Tim Pütz](41501290.md), but withdrew from the match. He reached the quarterfinals of the Masters 1000 in [Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), losing to eventual tournament winner Rafael Nadal.[^cite_note-145] In his second Grand Slam tournament of the year – in [Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), where he was seeded No. 6 – he won against [Oscar Otte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_Otte), [Roman Safiullin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Safiullin), [Laslo Đere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laslo_%C4%90ere), [Kei Nishikori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kei_Nishikori) and [Alejandro Davidovich Fokina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alejandro_Davidovich_Fokina). In the semifinal, he played Stefanos Tsitsipas, to whom he lost after a 5-set battle.[^cite_note-146]

Zverev's first appearance on grass started with a tournament at home in [Halle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Halle_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles) – he lost in the second round to the eventual tournament winner [Ugo Humbert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ugo_Humbert). At [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), seeded No. 4, he equaled his best achievement at the tournament to date – reaching the fourth round for a second time. Along the way he defeated [Tallon Griekspoor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tallon_Griekspoor), [Tennys Sandgren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennys_Sandgren) and [Taylor Fritz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Fritz). He was defeated in the fourth round by [Félix Auger-Aliassime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F%C3%A9lix_Auger-Aliassime) in five sets. Following this run he returned to the top 5 after 2 years on 12 July 2021. At the [Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_at_the_2020_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Zverev beat [Yen-hsun Lu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yen-hsun_Lu), [Daniel Elahi Galán](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Elahi_Gal%C3%A1n), [Nikoloz Basilashvili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikoloz_Basilashvili) and [Jérémy Chardy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%A9r%C3%A9my_Chardy) to reach the semifinals. In the semifinals, despite being a set and a break down against world No. 1 [Novak Djokovic](16100029.md), Zverev rallied to break back and won eight games in a row on his way to win in three sets.[^cite_note-147][^cite_note-148] He defeated [Karen Khachanov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_Khachanov) in straight sets in the final to claim the Olympic gold medal.[^cite_note-149] Zverev became the first German man to win a gold medal in singles and the first to win a medal since [Tommy Haas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tommy_Haas) won his silver medal at the 2000 Sydney Olympics.[^cite_note-150]

Following the Olympics, Zverev reached his second Masters 1000 final of the year at the [2021 Western & Southern Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Western_&_Southern_Open) after defeating [Lloyd Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Harris_\(tennis\)), [Guido Pella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guido_Pella), [Casper Ruud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casper_Ruud) and [Stefanos Tsitsipas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefanos_Tsitsipas) after a close three-set battle in the semifinal. Zverev had not won a match at the tournament in six prior appearances.[^cite_note-151] He beat [Andrey Rublev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrey_Rublev) for the 17th title of his career and the fourth of the season, in 59 minutes, the shortest match in the tournament history.[^cite_note-152] At the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Zverev sought to claim his first major title following his run to the final the previous year. He reached the semifinals following wins over [Jack Sock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Sock) and [Lloyd Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Harris_\(tennis\)). There, he lost to Novak Djokovic in five sets despite taking the first set, ending his career-high 16-match win streak stretching back to the Olympics.[^cite_note-153]

Zverev was seeded 3rd at the [2021 BNP Paribas Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), where he reached the quarterfinals. On the way, he defeated [Jenson Brooksby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jenson_Brooksby), [Andy Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Murray) and [Gaël Monfils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ga%C3%ABl_Monfils). He lost to [Taylor Fritz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Fritz), despite having two match points.[^cite_note-154] At the [Erste Bank Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erste_Bank_Open) in Vienna, seeded second, he defeated [Filip Krajinović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filip_Krajinovi%C4%87) and [Alex de Minaur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_de_Minaur) to reach the quarterfinals. The victory over De Minaur gave Zverev his 300th ATP tour match win.[^cite_note-155] He then beat [Félix Auger-Aliassime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F%C3%A9lix_Auger-Aliassime) and [Carlos Alcaraz](63121147.md) to reach the final where he won his fifth title of the year and 18th overall, defeating [Frances Tiafoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frances_Tiafoe) in straight sets.[^cite_note-156]

Zverev was the fourth seed at the [2021 Rolex Paris Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Rolex_Paris_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles). He received a bye into the second round, where he defeated [Dušan Lajović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Du%C5%A1an_Lajovi%C4%87). He defeated next sixteenth seed [Grigor Dimitrov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grigor_Dimitrov) in the third round and sixth seed [Casper Ruud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casper_Ruud) in the quarterfinals. He lost in the semifinals to second seed [Daniil Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniil_Medvedev) in straight sets. As a result of this run he equaled his singles career-high ranking of World No. 3 on 8 November 2021. For a fifth successive season, Zverev qualified for the [2021 ATP Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_ATP_Finals) in Turin, as the third seed. He began his campaign by defeating home favourite [Matteo Berrettini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matteo_Berrettini) who retired with an injury.[^cite_note-157] On his way to the title he defeated the top two seeds [Djokovic](16100029.md) and [Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniil_Medvedev) in the semifinals and finals respectively.[^cite_note-158] Due to this win, he became the only player active to have won multiple [ATP Finals](1209231.md) titles (2) other than Djokovic and Roger Federer, with 7 and 6, respectively.

### 2022: World No. 2, ankle injury



Zverev at the [2022 Monte-Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Monte-Carlo_Masters)

Zverev started his 2022 season by representing Germany at the [ATP Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_ATP_Cup). Germany was in Group C alongside Great Britain, the US, and Canada. Against Great Britain, he beat [Cameron Norrie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameron_Norrie). Great Britain ended up winning the tie over Germany 2–1.[^cite_note-159] Against the US, he defeated [Taylor Fritz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Fritz). Germany won the tie 2–1 over the USA.[^cite_note-160] Against Canada, he lost to [Félix Auger-Aliassime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F%C3%A9lix_Auger-Aliassime) in three sets. Canada won the tie over Germany 2–1.[^cite_note-161] Germany not only failed to qualify for the semi-finals, but they also ended third in Group C. Seeded third at the [Australian Open](69399815.md), he reached the fourth round where he was defeated by 14th seed, [Denis Shapovalov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denis_Shapovalov), in straight sets.[^cite_note-162][^cite_note-163]

After the Australian Open, Zverev played at the [Open Sud de France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Open_Sud_de_France_%E2%80%93_Singles). As the top seed, he made it to the final where he lost to sixth seed [Alexander Bublik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Bublik).[^cite_note-164] Seeded second at the [Mexican Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Abierto_Mexicano_Telcel_%E2%80%93_Singles), Zverev played in what was the record latest match ever played, defeating [Jenson Brooksby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jenson_Brooksby) at 4\:55 A.M.[^cite_note-165] He was then defaulted from the tournament ahead of his following match against compatriot, [Peter Gojowczyk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Gojowczyk), for violently and repeatedly hitting the umpire's chair with his racket after his loss in doubles.[^cite_note-Laughton-166] Representing Germany in the [Davis Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Davis_Cup_qualifying_round) tie against Brazil, he won both of his matches by beating [Thiago Seyboth Wild](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thiago_Seyboth_Wild) and [Thiago Monteiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thiago_Monteiro_\(tennis\)).[^cite_note-167][^cite_note-168] Germany ended up winning the tie over Brazil 3–1 to qualify for the Davis Cup Finals.[^cite_note-169] Seeded third at the [Indian Wells Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he was stunned in his second-round match by [Tommy Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tommy_Paul_\(tennis\)).[^cite_note-170] However, in [doubles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_doubles), he and partner, [Andrey Golubev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrey_Golubev), reached the semifinals where they lost to eventual champions [John Isner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Isner) and [Jack Sock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Sock).[^cite_note-171] Seeded second at the [Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he reached the quarterfinals where he lost to sixth seed, world No. 8, and eventual finalist, [Casper Ruud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casper_Ruud), in three sets.[^cite_note-172]

Zverev started his clay-court season at the [Monte-Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Monte-Carlo_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles). Seeded second, he reached the semifinals after beating ninth seed and world No. 12, Jannik Sinner, in the quarterfinals in a three-set thriller.[^cite_note-173] He lost in the semifinals to third seed, world No. 5, defending champion, and eventual champion, [Stefanos Tsitsipas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefanos_Tsitsipas), in straight sets.[^cite_note-174] As the top seed at the [BMW Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_BMW_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles) in Munich, he was upset in the second round by world No. 70 and eventual champion, [Holger Rune](60992715.md).[^cite_note-175] Seeded second at the [Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he returned to defend his title. He reached the semifinals with a straight set win over eighth seed and world No. 10, [Félix Auger-Aliassime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F%C3%A9lix_Auger-Aliassime), in the quarterfinals,[^cite_note-176] after a tough three sets match win over [Marin Čilić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marin_%C4%8Cili%C4%87) in the second round[^cite_note-177] and [Lorenzo Musetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lorenzo_Musetti)'s retirement in the third round due to a leg injury.[^cite_note-178] In the semifinals, he took revenge on fourth seed, world No. 5, two-time Monte-Carlo champion, and 2019 finalist, Stefanos Tsitsipas, to reach his third final at this Masters 1000 event. He improved his ATP Head2Head record to 4–7 against Tsitsipas, earning his first clay-court win against him in the process.[^cite_note-179] In the final, he was defeated by seventh seed and world No. 9, [Carlos Alcaraz](63121147.md).[^cite_note-180] Seeded second at the [Italian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he reached the semifinals defeating [Cristian Garín](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cristian_Gar%C3%ADn) in the quarterfinals.[^cite_note-181] In the semifinals, he faced fourth seed, Stefanos Tsitsipas, again for the third consecutive time at this Masters level in the clay court season.[^cite_note-182] He lost his semifinal match to Tsitsipas in three sets.[^cite_note-183]

Seeded third at the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Zverev matched his semifinal result from the previous year, defeating sixth seed, Carlos Alcaraz, in the quarterfinals in what was his first top-10 victory at a major after 12 attempts.[^cite_note-184] In his semifinal match against fifth seed, former world No. 1, and 13 time Roland Garros champion, [Rafael Nadal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Nadal), he retired 3 hours and 13 minutes into the match after [rolling his right ankle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sprained_ankle) and tearing all three lateral ligaments.[^cite_note-ATP_surgery-185] He was rolled off the court in a wheelchair.[^cite_note-186] Zverev informed that the injury would cause him to miss the [2022 Wimbledon Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Wimbledon_Championships)[^cite_note-187] and on 8 June 2022 underwent surgery to repair the torn ligaments in his ankle.[^cite_note-ATP_surgery-185] Despite his exit at Roland Garros, he reached a career-high ranking of world No. 2 on 13 June 2022.[^cite_note-188] In September, he was due to return for the [Davis Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Davis_Cup) play but suffered a new injury, a bone edema, and withdrew from competition for the rest of the season.[^cite_note-189] Zverev ended the year ranked 12, his lowest in five years, when he debuted in the top 10 and remained a fixture there.[^cite_note-190]

### 2023: French Open semifinals, Hamburg title

&#x20;Zverev returned to the tour for the first time since his ankle injury by participating at the inaugural [United Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_United_Cup) where he lost in straight sets to [Jiří Lehečka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ji%C5%99%C3%AD_Lehe%C4%8Dka) and [Taylor Fritz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Fritz).[^cite_note-191][^cite_note-192] Seeded 12th at the [Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he won his first match since June 2022 by beating lucky loser, [Juan Pablo Varillas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Pablo_Varillas), in the first round in five sets.[^cite_note-193] He was defeated in the second round by American and lucky loser, [Michael Mmoh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Mmoh), in four sets.[^cite_note-194]



Zverev signing autographs for fans at the [2023 Mutua Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Mutua_Madrid_Open)

After the Australian Open, Zverev represented Germany in the [Davis Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Davis_Cup_qualifying_round) tie against Switzerland. He won his first match over [Stan Wawrinka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stan_Wawrinka).[^cite_note-195][^cite_note-196] He lost his second match to [Marc-Andrea Hüsler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marc-Andrea_H%C3%BCsler).[^cite_note-197] Switzerland ended up winning the tie over Germany 3–2.[^cite_note-198] Seeded eighth at the [Rotterdam Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_ABN_AMRO_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), he was eliminated from the tournament in the second round by Dutch wildcard, [Tallon Griekspoor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tallon_Griekspoor), in three sets.[^cite_note-199] Seeded fourth at the [Qatar ExxonMobil Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Qatar_ExxonMobil_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), he fell in the second round to wildcard, former world No. 1, two-time champion, and eventual finalist, [Andy Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Murray).[^cite_note-200] In March, he played at the [Dubai Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Dubai_Tennis_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles). Seeded seventh, he reached the semifinals where he lost to second seed, defending champion, and eventual finalist, [Andrey Rublev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrey_Rublev).[^cite_note-201] Zverev began his early U.S. hard court campaign at the [Indian Wells Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles). Seeded 12th, he reached the fourth round but lost to fifth seed, world No. 6, and eventual finalist, [Daniil Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniil_Medvedev), in three close sets.[^cite_note-202] Seeded 13th at the [Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he was beaten in the second round by wildcard [Taro Daniel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taro_Daniel).[^cite_note-203]

Zverev began his clay-court season at the [Monte-Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Monte-Carlo_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles). Seeded 13th, he earned his first victory over [Alexander Bublik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Bublik) by beating him in the first round in three sets.[^cite_note-204] In the second round, he defeated world No. 29, [Roberto Bautista Agut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Bautista_Agut), in the second round.[^cite_note-205] In the third round, he lost to third seed, Daniil Medvedev, despite serving for the match twice and having two match points in the third-set tie-break.[^cite_note-206] This defeat was his seventh out of the last eight matches played with Medvedev. Seeded third at the [BMW Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_BMW_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles) in Munich, he was defeated in the second round by [Christopher O'Connell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_O'Connell).[^cite_note-207] Seeded 13th at the [Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he recorded his 350th career win by defeating [Roberto Carballés Baena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Carball%C3%A9s_Baena) in the second round, becoming the only player under 30 to reach this milestone.[^cite_note-208][^cite_note-209] In the third round, he overpowered qualifier, [Hugo Grenier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugo_Grenier), in 55 minutes.[^cite_note-210] In the fourth round, he lost to top seed, world No. 2, defending champion, and eventual champion, [Carlos Alcaraz](63121147.md), in a rematch of the [previous year's final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles).[^cite_note-211] Failing to defend his finalist points from last year, his ranking fell from 16 to 22. Seeded 19th at the [Italian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he made it to the fourth round where he lost to third seed and eventual champion, Daniil Medvedev.[^cite_note-212] Having made the semifinals last year, his ranking again fell, this time from 22 to 27. Seeded third at the [Geneva Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Geneva_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), he reached the semifinal where he lost to [Nicolás Jarry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicol%C3%A1s_Jarry), who would end up winning the tournament.[^cite_note-213] Seeded 22nd at the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he defeated [Lloyd Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Harris_\(tennis\)), [Alex Molčan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Mol%C4%8Dan), 12th seed [Frances Tiafoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frances_Tiafoe), and 28th seed [Grigor Dimitrov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grigor_Dimitrov) in his first four rounds.[^cite_note-214][^cite_note-215][^cite_note-216][^cite_note-217] In the quarterfinals, he faced [Tomás Martín Etcheverry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom%C3%A1s_Mart%C3%ADn_Etcheverry). He prevailed over Etcheverry in four sets to reach his third consecutive semi-final at this tournament.[^cite_note-218] He fell in his semi-final match to world No. 4 and last year finalist, [Casper Ruud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casper_Ruud), in straight sets.[^cite_note-219]

Zverev began his grass season by withdrawing from the [BOSS Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_BOSS_Open) in Stuttgart due to a thigh injury that he picked up during his semifinal match at Roland Garros.[^cite_note-220][^cite_note-221] Zverev then defeated [Dominic Thiem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominic_Thiem), [Denis Shapovalov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denis_Shapovalov), and [Nicolas Jarry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicol%C3%A1s_Jarry) in [Halle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Halle_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles) to reach the semifinals where he lost to eventual champion [Alexander Bublik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Bublik).[^cite_note-222] At [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Wimbledon_Championships), Zverev lost in third round to [Matteo Berrettini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matteo_Berrettini).[^cite_note-223] In [Båstad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Swedish_Open), Zverev lost in the quarterfinals to Andrey Rublev.[^cite_note-224] Zverev won the [Hamburg European Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Hamburg_European_Open), defeating [Laslo Djere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laslo_Djere) in the final. His triumph marked his 20th career ATP Title.[^cite_note-225]

During his [2023 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_US_Open_\(tennis\)) match against Jannik Sinner, Zverev halted play and complained to officials in response to a fan shouting the opening stanza to "Deutschlandlied", a song which has served as the national anthem of Germany; the use of the first and second stanza are presently commonly discouraged because of history. Alexander Zverev interpreted this as carrying Nazi sentiment and told officials that the fan had shouted, "the most famous Hitler phrase".[^cite_note-226] Zverev lost the quarterfinals match against Carlos Alcaraz after winning a 4-hour 40 minutes five-set match against Jannik Sinner.[^cite_note-227] This match, in combination with the Roland Garros 2023 semi-final, cemented Zverev's return to top form.[^cite_note-228][^cite_note-229]

His performance in the Asian swing was marked by his 21st title in [Chengdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Chengdu_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), defeating [Roman Safiullin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Safiullin) in three sets.[^cite_note-230] He followed this result with a semifinals showing in [Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_China_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), losing to [Danill Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniil_Medvedev) in their 5th meeting of the year. Unable to maintain his form, he lost in the second round of the [Shanghai Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Rolex_Shanghai_Masters) against [Roman Safiullin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Safiullin),[^cite_note-231] where he was the defending finalist from [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Rolex_Shanghai_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles), and lost to [Jordan Thompson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Thompson_\(tennis\)) in [Tokyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Japan_Open_Tennis_Championships_%E2%80%93_Singles).[^cite_note-232] During the European indoor hardcourt season, Zverev made the quarterfinals in [Vienna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Erste_Bank_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles) before losing to [Andrey Rublev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrey_Rublev) in three sets and reached the third round in [Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Rolex_Paris_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles), losing to [Stefanos Tsitsipas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefanos_Tsitsipas).[^cite_note-233][^cite_note-234] Despite this, he qualified for the [ATP Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_ATP_Finals_%E2%80%93_Singles) for the first time since [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_ATP_Finals_%E2%80%93_Singles).[^cite_note-235] At the event, he was drawn in the red group and defeated [Carlos Alcaraz](63121147.md) in three sets and [Andrey Rublev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrey_Rublev) in straight sets whilst losing to Medvedev.[^cite_note-236] He failed to qualify for the semifinals after Alcaraz and Medvedev were more successful in terms of win-loss percentage in sets, ending his 2023 season.[^cite_note-237]

### 2024: United Cup champion, two Masters titles and French Open final

Alongside Team Germany, Zverev competed and won the United Cup, saving two championship points in his singles match against [Hubert Hurkacz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hubert_Hurkacz) and defeating Team Poland in the final.[^cite_note-238] In [Melbourne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he reached the quarterfinals of the [2024 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), defeating [Dominik Koepfer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominik_Koepfer), [Lukas Klein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luk%C3%A1%C5%A1_Klein) for his 400th career win,[^cite_note-239] [Alex Michelsen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_Michelsen), and [Cameron Norrie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameron_Norrie), with two matches requiring deciding set tiebreakers.[^cite_note-240] He then achieved his first-ever win against a top-5 player at a Grand Slam, defeating [Carlos Alcaraz](63121147.md) in four sets in the quarterfinals, to make his first Australian Open semifinal appearance since [2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles).[^cite_note-241] He subsequently lost to [Danill Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniil_Medvedev), despite being two sets to love up. His defeat marked only his second loss from being up two sets to love in a 5-set match, his first being against [Dominic Thiem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominic_Thiem) in the final of the [2020 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles).[^cite_note-242]

In Mexico, Zverev reached the semifinals in [Los Cabos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Los_Cabos_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles) with straight sets wins against [Yoshihito Nishoka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoshihito_Nishioka) and [Thanasi Kokkinakis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thanasi_Kokkinakis) but lost to [Jordan Thompson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Thompson_\(tennis\)) in a deciding set tiebreaker.[^cite_note-243] In [Acapulco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Abierto_Mexicano_Telcel_%E2%80%93_Singles), Zverev lost to [Daniel Altmaier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Altmaier) in the first round.[^cite_note-244] Seeded sixth at the [BNP Paribas Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_BNP_Paribas_Open), Zverev reached the quarterfinals for the first time since [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_BNP_Paribas_Open), defeating [Christopher O'Connell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_O'Connell), [Tallon Griekspoor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tallon_Griekspoor), and [Alex de Minaur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_de_Minaur)[^cite_note-245] before losing to eventual champion [Carlos Alcaraz](63121147.md).[^cite_note-246][^cite_note-247] Seeded fourth at the [Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Miami_Open), Zverev reached the semifinals, defeating [Félix Auger-Aliassime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F%C3%A9lix_Auger-Aliassime), [Christopher Eubanks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Eubanks), [Karen Khachanov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_Khachanov), and [Fábián Marozsán](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F%C3%A1bi%C3%A1n_Marozs%C3%A1n)[^cite_note-248][^cite_note-249] before losing to [Grigor Dimitrov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grigor_Dimitrov).[^cite_note-250] This was his best result in Miami since reaching the final in [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Miami_Open).[^cite_note-251]

Zverev began his 2024 clay season seeded fifth at the [Monte-Carlo Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Monte-Carlo_Masters), receiving a bye into the second round and defeating [Sebastian Ofner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebastian_Ofner) before losing to eventual champion [Stefanos Tsitsipas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefanos_Tsitsipas) in the third round.[^cite_note-252][^cite_note-253] As the top seed at the [BMW Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_BMW_Open), Zverev received a bye into the second round and beat [Jurij Rodionov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jurij_Rodionov) before being upset by world No. 106 [Cristian Garín](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cristian_Gar%C3%ADn) in the quarterfinals.[^cite_note-254][^cite_note-255] Seeded fourth in [Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Mutua_Madrid_Open), Zverev reached the fourth round, beating [Borna Ćorić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borna_%C4%86ori%C4%87) and [Denis Shapovalov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denis_Shapovalov) before being upset by 21st seed [Francisco Cerúndolo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Cer%C3%BAndolo).[^cite_note-256][^cite_note-257] Seeded third in [Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Italian_Open_\(tennis\)), Zverev won his sixth Masters title, defeating [Aleksandar Vukic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksandar_Vukic), [Nuno Borges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuno_Borges_\(tennis\)), [Taylor Fritz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Fritz), [Alejandro Tabilo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alejandro_Tabilo), and [Nicolás Jarry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicol%C3%A1s_Jarry) in the final. This was his best result in Rome since winning his first Masters 1000 title at the [2017 Italian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Italian_Open_\(tennis\)), and his first Masters title since 2021.[^cite_note-258][^cite_note-259]

At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Zverev contested Rafael Nadal's final match at the French Open, defeating the 14-time champion in straight sets to record Nadal's fourth ever defeat at the event.[^cite_note-260] He went on to reach the semifinals, winning over [David Goffin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Goffin), [Tallon Griekspoor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tallon_Griekspoor), [Holger Rune](60992715.md), and [Alex de Minaur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_de_Minaur).[^cite_note-261][^cite_note-262][^cite_note-263] He then defeated two-time finalist [Casper Ruud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casper_Ruud) to reach his first Major final since the [2020 US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) and his first on clay.[^cite_note-264][^cite_note-265] In the deciding set of the final match, Alcaraz hit a second serve which was called out by the line judges. However, the chair umpire overruled the call, declaring the ball in. While electronic review later indicated the serve was out, Roland Garros does not use Hawk-Eye as an official officiating tool; instead, the technology is used exclusively for television broadcasts.[^cite_note-266] It is noted that the broadcast version of Hawk-Eye operates with significantly lower precision than the official Electronic Line Calling (ELC) systems installed at other tournaments, leaving open the possibility that the umpire's ruling on the clay mark was accurate. However, the overrule generated controversy. Had the call not been overturned, Zverev would have broken back. Tennis commentator [Robbie Koenig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robbie_Koenig) and former world No. 1 [Andy Roddick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Roddick) disagreed with the overrule.[^cite_note-267][^cite_note-268] Zverev would go on to lose the fifth set, giving up his two-sets-to-one lead and finishing runner-up to [Carlos Alcaraz](63121147.md), who collected his maiden French Open title.[^cite_note-269]

In [Halle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Halle_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), Zverev reached the semifinals after collecting wins against [Oscar Otte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_Otte),[^cite_note-270] [Lorenzo Sonego](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lorenzo_Sonego),[^cite_note-271] and [Arthur Fils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_Fils),[^cite_note-272] losing to [Hubert Hurkacz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hubert_Hurkacz) in straight sets.[^cite_note-273] At [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Wimbledon_Championships), Zverev beat [Roberto Carballés Baena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Carball%C3%A9s_Baena), [Marcos Giron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcos_Giron), and [Cameron Norrie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameron_Norrie) before losing to [Taylor Fritz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Fritz) in the fourth round.[^cite_note-274] His third-set tiebreak with Norrie was noted as one of the longest tiebreaks of the tournament, with Zverev prevailing 17–15 after 26 minutes.[^cite_note-275] Back on clay, Zverev reached the final in [Hamburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Hamburg_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), losing to [Arthur Fils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_Fils) in three sets.[^cite_note-276] At the [Olympic Games in Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tennis_at_the_2024_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Zverev represented [Team Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germany_at_the_2024_Summer_Olympics) as defending champion. He progressed to the quarterfinals after winning over [Jaume Munar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaume_Munar) of Spain,[^cite_note-277] [Tomáš Macháč](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomas_Machac) of Czech Republic,[^cite_note-278] and [Alexei Popyrin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexei_Popyrin) of Australia,[^cite_note-279] all in straight sets. He was defeated in the quarterfinals by eventual bronze medalist [Lorenzo Musetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lorenzo_Musetti) of Italy in straight sets.[^cite_note-280]

In the American hard court swing, Zverev reached the quarterfinals in [Montréal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_National_Bank_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), losing to [Sebastian Korda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebastian_Korda) in three sets.[^cite_note-281] He went on to defend his semifinal points in [Cincinnati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Cincinnati_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), losing to Jannik Sinner in an attritive three-set match.[^cite_note-282] At the [US Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he reached the quarterfinals without facing a seeded opponent, with wins over lucky loser [Maximilian Marterer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maximilian_Marterer),[^cite_note-283] wildcard [Alexandre Müller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandre_M%C3%BCller),[^cite_note-284] [Tomás Martín Etcheverry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom%C3%A1s_Mart%C3%ADn_Etcheverry)[^cite_note-285] and [Brandon Nakashima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brandon_Nakashima)[^cite_note-286] — his 450th career win. With that milestone, he became the second player born in the 1990s, after [Grigor Dimitrov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grigor_Dimitrov), to reach 450 ATP wins.[^cite_note-287] With the win, he qualified for his seventh appearance at the [2024 ATP Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_ATP_Finals).[^cite_note-288] Zverev lost in the quarterfinals to [Taylor Fritz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Fritz).[^cite_note-289]

At the [Paris Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Rolex_Paris_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles), Zverev defeated [Tallon Griekspoor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tallon_Griekspoor), [Arthur Fils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_Fils), and [Stefanos Tsitsipas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefanos_Tsitsipas) to set up a semifinal clash with former champion [Holger Rune](60992715.md).
