He won the match in straight sets to reach the final for the second time at this event. Zverev defeated home crowd favorite [Ugo Humbert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ugo_Humbert) in the final[^cite_note-290] to clinch his second Masters title of the season and the world No. 2 ranking. Zverev won all three of his group matches at the [ATP Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_ATP_Finals_%E2%80%93_Singles) to reach the semifinals,[^cite_note-291] where he lost to [Taylor Fritz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Fritz) in a deciding set tie-break.[^cite_note-292]

### 2025: Australian Open final, 500th win

Alongside Team Germany, Zverev failed to defend the United Cup after withdrawing from the event due to injury. Entering the [2025 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) as the second seed for the first time in his career, Zverev began his tournament with a dominant victory over 2019 semifinalist [Lucas Pouille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucas_Pouille) in the first round.[^cite_note-293] He reached his second consecutive and third overall semifinal at the Australian Open defeating [Pedro Martínez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedro_Mart%C3%ADnez_\(tennis\)), [Jacob Fearnley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacob_Fearnley), [Ugo Humbert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ugo_Humbert), and [Tommy Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tommy_Paul_\(tennis\)). Against the 24-time major champion, Zverev won the 81-minute first set against Djokovic before the latter's retirement due to injury, advancing to his third major final.[^cite_note-294] In the final, Zverev lost to defending champion Jannik Sinner in straight sets.[^cite_note-295] Following his defeat to Sinner, a visibly distraught Zverev delivered an emotional speech during the trophy ceremony, stating, "I want to thank my team... I'm just not good enough. It's as simple as that." In the post-match press conference, he reflected on his career trajectory and the pressure of being a perennial contender without a Major title, reportedly remarking that he "didn't want to be the best player in history without a Grand Slam",[^cite_note-296][^cite_note-297][^cite_note-298] expressing his desperation to finally break the barrier after losing his third major final.[^cite_note-299]

Over the next two months, Zverev made quarterfinals at the South American events he played in, [Buenos Aires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Argentina_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles) and [Rio de Janeiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Rio_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), losing to Argentines [Francisco Cerundolo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Cer%C3%BAndolo) and [Francisco Comesana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Comesa%C3%B1a), respectively.[^cite_note-300][^cite_note-301] Zverev then made only modest achievements in the succeeding weeks, losing to [Learner Tien](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Learner_Tien) in the second round of the [Mexican Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Abierto_Mexicano_Telcel_%E2%80%93_Singles),[^cite_note-302] to [Tallon Griekspoor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tallon_Griekspoor) in the second round of [Indian Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles),[^cite_note-303] to [Arthur Fils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_Fils) in the fourth round of the [Miami Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles),[^cite_note-304] and to [Matteo Berrettini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matteo_Berrettini) in [Monte-Carlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Monte-Carlo_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles).[^cite_note-305]

In April, Zverev won his 3rd title in [Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_BMW_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles) and 24th overall on his birthday, winning in straight sets over Ben Shelton. Zverev was presented his trophy BMW by [Dominic Thiem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominic_Thiem),[^cite_note-306] and reclaimed the world No. 2 ranking from Alcaraz.[^cite_note-307][^cite_note-308] The next week, he reached the round of 16 at the [Madrid Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles). During his third round match, Zverev was given a code violation for taking a photo of the ball mark of shot which Zverev considered to have been erroneously called in.[^cite_note-309] Later, social media posts from the ATP Tour suggested that the Hawk-Eye call was correct and that Zverev likely took a picture of a different mark.[^cite_note-310] Following this, Zverev failed to defend either his titles in [Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) and [Hamburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Hamburg_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) with a straight sets loss against an in-form [Lorenzo Musetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lorenzo_Musetti) and a three-set loss against [Alexandre Müller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandre_M%C3%BCller), respectively.[^cite_note-311][^cite_note-312] At the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) where he was defending finalist points, Zverev advanced to the quarterfinals, where he was defeated by [Novak Djokovic](16100029.md) in a four-set match.

On the grass, Zverev reached his third final of the year in [Stuttgart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_BOSS_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), losing to [Taylor Fritz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Fritz) in straight sets in his fifth consecutive loss against the American.[^cite_note-313] He then reached the semifinals against [Daniil Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniil_Medvedev) in [Halle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Halle_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), losing in three sets.[^cite_note-314] Zverev then lost in the first round of [Wimbledon](80007709.md) to [Arthur Rinderknech](57494697.md) in five sets, which marked the first time he lost in the first round of a major since [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles).[^cite_note-315] Zverev reached the semifinals for the first time in [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_National_Bank_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) since [winning the title in 2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Rogers_Cup_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) after defeating the defending champion [Alexei Popyrin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexei_Popyrin) in the quarterfinals.[^cite_note-316] He lost to the eventual finalist [Karen Khachanov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_Khachanov), despite holding a match point.[^cite_note-317] He rebounded in [Cincinnati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Cincinnati_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) by advancing to his 3rd consecutive semifinal in the American Midwest, which included wins against the recent finalists from Toronto the week before, Khachanov and [Ben Shelton](71310148.md), both of whom were notably exhausted from their Canadian campaigns.[^cite_note-318][^cite_note-319] Zverev, also struggling and exhausted in his semifinal against Carlos Alcaraz, went on to lose in straight sets.[^cite_note-320] In [New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he was defeated in four sets by a resurgent [Félix Auger-Aliassime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F%C3%A9lix_Auger-Aliassime).[^cite_note-321] Before leaving the United States, Zverev participated in the [Laver Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Laver_Cup) with Team Europe in San Francisco but lost to Team World.[^cite_note-\:1-322]

In the Asian swing, Zverev was defeated by frequent rival [Daniil Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniil_Medvedev) in the quarterfinals in [Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_China_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles),[^cite_note-323] and by eventual finalist [Arthur Rinderknech](57494697.md) in the third round in [Shanghai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Rolex_Shanghai_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles).[^cite_note-324] Zverev returned to form in the European indoor swing, but found himself obstructed by Jannik Sinner for the remainder of the season, losing to him in the final in [Vienna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Erste_Bank_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles),[^cite_note-325] the semifinals of [Nanterre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Rolex_Paris_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles),[^cite_note-326] and a round-robin match in [Turin](81434533.md).[^cite_note-327] At the [Davis Cup Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Davis_Cup_Finals), Zverev won both of his singles matches, but [Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germany_Davis_Cup_team) was defeated in the semifinal tie against Spain.[^cite_note-328] He ended his season ranked world No. 3 behind Sinner and Alcaraz.

### 2026: French Open and US Open champion

Main article: [2026 Alexander Zverev tennis season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Alexander_Zverev_tennis_season)



Zverev serving against [Jesper de Jong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesper_de_Jong) at the [French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_French_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles).

For the second consecutive year, Zverev and Team Germany failed to progress at the [United Cup](81647045.md).[^cite_note-\:2-329] At the [2026 Australian Open](81715568.md), Zverev progressed to his third consecutive semifinal, defeating [Cameron Norrie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameron_Norrie), [Francisco Cerúndolo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Cer%C3%BAndolo), and [Learner Tien](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Learner_Tien) en route. In a rematch of their quarterfinal match in 2024, Zverev lost to eventual champion [Carlos Alcaraz](63121147.md) in a five-set epic semifinal lasting five hours and 27 minutes, ranking as the third longest match in Australian Open history.[^cite_note-330] During the semifinal, Zverev dropped the first two sets, but rebounded to win the next two in consecutive tiebreaks to push the match to a deciding set, the first deciding set of the men's tournament on [Rod Laver Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rod_Laver_Arena). In the fifth set, Zverev had an opportunity to serve for the match, but was broken twice to lose the match. Failing to defend his finalist points from [2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), he dropped to world No. 4 in the rankings, behind [Novak Djokovic](16100029.md).

In [Acapulco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Abierto_Mexicano_Telcel_%E2%80%93_Singles), Zverev lost in the second round of singles to [Miomir Kecmanovic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miomir_Kecmanovi%C4%87).[^cite_note-331] However, in doubles, partnered with [Marcelo Melo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcelo_Melo), the pair took the doubles title, defeating [Alexander Erler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Erler) & [Robert Galloway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Galloway_\(tennis\)) in the final.[^cite_note-332] Zverev then made consecutive Masters 1000 semifinals at [Indian Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_BNP_Paribas_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles),[^cite_note-333] [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Miami_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles),[^cite_note-334] and [Monte-Carlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Monte-Carlo_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles),[^cite_note-335] improving significantly on his results from the previous year. By reaching his first Indian Wells semifinal, he completed the semifinal set of all nine Masters 1000 tournaments, the first person outside of the [Big Four](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Three_\(tennis\)) to do so.[^cite_note-336] He went on to lose all three semifinals to the eventual champion of all three events, Jannik Sinner, failing to win a set in any of the three matches and extending his ongoing losing streak to eight consecutive losses against the Italian.[^cite_note-337][^cite_note-338][^cite_note-339] Zverev then failed to defend the title in [Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_BMW_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), being defeated by [Flavio Cobolli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flavio_Cobolli) in the semifinals. In [Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Mutua_Madrid_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), Zverev advanced to his fourth final in the [Magic Box](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caja_M%C3%A1gica), a first Masters 1000 final of the season for a chance at a third Madrid Open title. He went on to lose to Sinner again in straight sets. The following week, he was upset by home favourite [Luciano Darderi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luciano_Darderi) at the round of 16 of the [Italian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Italian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), ending his run of five successive Masters 1000 semifinals dating back to the [Paris Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Rolex_Paris_Masters_%E2%80%93_Singles).

At the [French Open](81641720.md), Zverev won his maiden major title. He entered the tournament as the second seed,[^cite_note-340] after two-time defending champion [Carlos Alcaraz](63121147.md) withdrew due to an ongoing wrist injury.[^cite_note-341] Following Jannik Sinner's abrupt loss to [Juan Manuel Cerundolo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Manuel_Cer%C3%BAndolo) and three-time French Open champion [Novak Djokovic's](16100029.md) loss to [Joāo Fonseca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo%C3%A3o_Fonseca_\(tennis\)),[^cite_note-342] Zverev was widely labelled the tournament favourite,[^cite_note-343] a label he distanced himself from as the tournament progressed.[^cite_note-straights-344][^cite_note-france-rg-26-345] By reaching his sixth consecutive quarterfinal appearance in Paris, Zverev joined [Roger Federer](262376.md), [Rafael Nadal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Nadal), and Djokovic as the only men's singles players to achieve this feat.[^cite_note-346] He then advanced to his second French Open final and fourth major final following wins against [Rafael Jódar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_J%C3%B3dar) in the quarterfinals and [Jakub Menšík](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jakub_Men%C5%A1%C3%ADk) in the semifinal.[^cite_note-347] In the final, Zverev defeated [Flavio Cobolli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flavio_Cobolli) in a five-set contest, his longest match of the tournament by duration and sets played.[^cite_note-348] With his win, Zverev became the first German men's singles champion since [Henner Henkel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henner_Henkel) in [1937](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1937_French_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles), ending an 89 year drought.[^cite_note-atp-facts-349] He became the first German male to win a major since [Boris Becker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris_Becker) in [1996](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_Australian_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) and joined [Michael Stich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Stich) and Becker to become the third German men's singles player to win a Major.[^cite_note-atp-facts-349] In addition, he became the first player, male or female, to win a Major with [Type 1 diabetes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Type_1_diabetes).[^cite_note-350]

Zverev rejoined the tour in [Halle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Halle_Open_%E2%80%93_Singles), reaching the semifinals just dropping one set to [Vit Kopřiva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V%C3%ADt_Kop%C5%99iva) in the second round.[^cite_note-351] He lost the semifinal match to eventual finalist [Taylor Fritz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Fritz) despite winning the first set, extending his ongoing losing streak against the American since [Wimbledon 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Wimbledon_Championships_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles) to seven consecutive losses.[^cite_note-352] During the [2026 Wimbledon](80434323.md), Zverev reached back-to-back second weeks of a major, and after defeating this time Taylor Fritz in the quarterfinal,[^cite_note-353] and [Arthur Fery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_Fery) in the semifinal, he made it to his second consecutive appearance in a Grand Slam final.[^cite_note-354]. In the final, he lost to Sinner in four sets.

Zverev lost his second round match to [Tallon Griekspoor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tallon_Griekspoor) at the [National Bank Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Bank_Open) in 3 sets, then lost his 4th round match at the [Cincinnati Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cincinnati_Masters) to [Tommy Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tommy_Paul_\(tennis\)) in 3 sets. During the [2026 US Open](84011000.md), Zverev entered as the first seed following Jannik Sinner's withdrawal due to a knee injury. He nearly suffered a first-round defeat, being 2 points away from losing to [Lorenzo Sonego](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lorenzo_Sonego) in the 4th set while down two sets to one, and serving at 4-5, 30-30. He came back to win the match in 5 sets, and won his second round match against [Quentin Halys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quentin_Halys) in five sets. Zverev then earned victories against [Alejandro Tabilo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alejandro_Tabilo), [Luciano Darderi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luciano_Darderi), [Botic van de Zandshulp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Botic_van_de_Zandschulp), and [Karen Khachanov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_Khachanov) without dropping a single set. He then won the final over [Ben Shelton](71310148.md) in four sets, winning his second major title of the season, and became the first German man to win the US Open since [Boris Becker in 1989](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men's_singles).

## Team competitions

### Laver Cup

Zverev took part in the inaugural [Laver Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Laver_Cup) in Prague in 2017. He accrued four points by winning both of his singles matches for Team Europe as they defeated Team World 15–9.[^cite_note-355] He played a more crucial role in [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Laver_Cup) and [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Laver_Cup), winning the clinching matches in both editions against Kevin Anderson and [Milos Raonic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milos_Raonic) respectively.[^cite_note-356][^cite_note-357] In the [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Laver_Cup) edition in Boston, he defeated [John Isner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Isner) in three sets in his only match in Team Europe's 14–1 win.[^cite_note-358][^cite_note-359] In [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Laver_Cup), Zverev returned to the Laver Cup after a two-year absence in which Team World had won both editions. He lost his first match to [Taylor Fritz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Fritz),[^cite_note-360] but rebounded on the third day to win against [Frances Tiafoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frances_Tiafoe) in a deciding match tie-break.[^cite_note-361] In [2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Laver_Cup), Zverev failed to win any matches against Team World, losing in straight sets to [Alex de Minaur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_de_Minaur) and [Taylor Fritz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Fritz), the latter contributing his sixth consecutive loss against the American and allowing Team World to secure their third Laver Cup title.[^cite_note-362][^cite_note-\:1-322] In [2026](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Laver_Cup), Zverev fell again to de Minaur, after being a set and a break up, but won the doubles with [Casper Ruud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casper_Ruud) later that day, and triumphed over [Learner Tien](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Learner_Tien) on the final day to return the Laver Cup to Team Europe, winning his first singles match at the event since 2024, and clinching the event for the fourth time.[^cite_note-363]

Notably, Zverev has played in a record seven Laver Cups, missing only the 2022 and 2023 editions. Additionally, of the seven he has played, Team Europe won the tournament on six of those occasions, meaning Zverev has been present every year Team Europe has won.[^cite_note-364] The 2025 Laver Cup was the only edition where Zverev played but Team Europe was not victorious.

### Davis Cup

Zverev made his Davis Cup debut for [Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germany_Davis_Cup_team) against the [Czech Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czech_Republic_Davis_Cup_team) in [2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Davis_Cup). He faced No. 7 [Tomáš Berdych](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom%C3%A1%C5%A1_Berdych) in his debut match and took a two sets to one lead before ultimately losing in five sets.[^cite_note-365] After the Czech Republic won the doubles rubber while Kohlschreiber won both singles matches, Zverev faced [Lukáš Rosol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luk%C3%A1%C5%A1_Rosol) in a decisive fifth rubber. Rosol won the match easily to send the Czech Republic into the next round.[^cite_note-366] In the [2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Davis_Cup) against [Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgium_Davis_Cup_team), Zverev recorded his first career match win in the competition against [Arthur De Greef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_De_Greef_\(tennis\)), but lost the doubles rubber with his brother as well as his second singles match to [Steve Darcis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Darcis). Belgium won the tie 4–1.[^cite_note-367] Zverev won his first [Davis Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Davis_Cup) tie in 2018, winning both of his singles matches against [Alex de Minaur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_de_Minaur) and Kyrgios to lead Germany to a 3–1 victory over [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia_Davis_Cup_team).[^cite_note-368] In the quarterfinals, Germany took a 2–1 lead against [Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spain_Davis_Cup_team) behind Zverev's win over [David Ferrer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Ferrer) and a victory in doubles. However, Spain ultimately won the tie on the final day after Zverev was unable to defeat Nadal and Kohlschreiber lost a tight five-set match to Ferrer.[^cite_note-369]

In [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Davis_Cup), the format of the Davis Cup was changed to have eighteen countries competing in the finals over a single week in November, all but six of which were decided through a qualifying round in February. Germany was placed in the qualifying round and drawn against [Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungary_Davis_Cup_team). Zverev participated and won both of his singles matches as Germany won the tie 5–0.[^cite_note-370] He did not participate in the finals in November, in which Germany lost in the quarterfinals.[^cite_note-371]

### Hopman Cup

Zverev represented Germany at the Hopman Cup for four consecutive years from 2016 through 2019 with three different partners. In [2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Hopman_Cup), he competed with [Sabine Lisicki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabine_Lisicki). The duo won their tie against the French team, with Zverev winning both his singles and mixed doubles matches.[^cite_note-372] However, they were shut out against Great Britain and Australia Green and did not advance to the final.[^cite_note-373][^cite_note-374] The [following year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Hopman_Cup), he entered the competition with [Andrea Petkovic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrea_Petkovic). While the pair only won their tie against Great Britain, Zverev also defeated Federer in singles in his first tournament back from injury.[^cite_note-375]

Zverev enjoyed more success in [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Hopman_Cup) and [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Hopman_Cup), pairing with [Angelique Kerber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angelique_Kerber). In their first year together, they advanced to the final, primarily on the strength of the pair winning all three of their mixed doubles matches.[^cite_note-376] In the final against Switzerland, Zverev lost his singles match to Federer.[^cite_note-377] Although Kerber won her singles match against [Belinda Bencic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belinda_Bencic), they lost the decisive mixed doubles rubber.[^cite_note-378] They returned in 2019 and again reached the final to set up a rematch of the previous year's final with Federer and Bencic. In a round-robin group with Australia, France, and Spain, the two of them won all six of their singles matches, but lost two of their three mixed doubles matches against Australia and France.[^cite_note-379] Like the previous year, the final was decided by the mixed doubles match after Federer defeated Zverev and Kerber defeated Bencic. With the [Fast4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fast4_Tennis) format, the match went to three sets. In the third set tiebreak, both teams had a match point at 4–4. Switzerland won the point with Federer serving to win the title.[^cite_note-380]

### ATP Cup

Zverev represented Germany at the [ATP Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATP_Cup) in [2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_ATP_Cup)[^cite_note-381] and [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_ATP_Cup).[^cite_note-382] At the debut of this tournament in 2020, he was on the team with Jan-Lennard Struff, Kevin Krawietz and Andreas Mies. In the group stage, where Germany met Australia, Canada and Greece, Zverev lost all three matches to Alex de Minaur, Denis Shapovalov and Stefanos Tsitsipas respectively. Germany did not advance to the next phase of the tournament, finishing third in the group.[^cite_note-383] In 2021, Germany played again with the same lineup as the previous year. In the group they played against Canada and Serbia. Zverev won his first match against Denis Shapovalov. In their second meeting, he lost in singles to Novak Djokovic, but still played against him and [Nikola Ćaćić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikola_%C4%86a%C4%87i%C4%87) in doubles while paired with Jan-Lennard Struff. Germany won that meeting, which allowed them to advance from first place in the group to the semifinals.[^cite_note-384] There they ended up against Russia, where Zverev lost his match to Daniil Medvedev. Germany eventually lost the tie 2–1, allowing Russia to advance to the final.[^cite_note-385]

### United Cup

Zverev has started his season every year since 2023 by participating in the United Cup. [In 2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_United_Cup), Zverev and Team Germany were placed in Group C alongside the Czech Republic and the USA. Against the Czech Republic, he lost to [Jiří Lehečka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ji%C5%99%C3%AD_Lehe%C4%8Dka).[^cite_note-386][^cite_note-387] The Czech Republic won the tie over Germany 3–2.[^cite_note-388] Against the US, he lost to [Taylor Fritz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Fritz).[^cite_note-389] The USA beat Germany 5–0.[^cite_note-390] Germany failed to qualify for the knockout round and ended third in Group C. [In 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_Cup), Zverev returned to the [United Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_Cup) in [Sydney](27862.md), [Australia](4689264.md) for the second consecutive year, leading Team Germany alongside [Angelique Kerber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angelique_Kerber).[^cite_note-391] He won both of his singles matches in the round-robin stage, defeating [Lorenzo Sonego](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lorenzo_Sonego) of [Italy](14532.md) and [Adrian Mannarino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adrian_Mannarino) of [France](5843419.md) both in three sets. He played both mixed doubles matches in both ties with Angelique Kerber, partnering for the first time since [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Hopman_Cup), and won against Team Italy but not Team France.[^cite_note-392][^cite_note-393]

Despite this, Team Germany managed to qualify as the best runner-up team from all countries participating in Sydney, advancing to the quarterfinals against [Greece](12108.md). There, Zverev decisively won his singles match against [Stefanos Tsitsipas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefanos_Tsitsipas) and his mixed doubles match, partnering with doubles specialist [Laura Siegemund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laura_Siegemund), against [Maria Sakkari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Sakkari) and [Petros Tsitsipas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petros_Tsitsipas).[^cite_note-394] In the semifinal tie against [Australia](4689264.md), Zverev was defeated by [Alex de Minaur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alex_de_Minaur) in three sets, allowing the Australian to crack the top 10 after previously making top 10 wins against [Taylor Fritz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Fritz) and [Novak Djokovic](16100029.md) earlier that week. He then participated in the mixed doubles match with Siegemund, against Australia's [Storm Hunter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Storm_Hunter) and [Matthew Ebden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthew_Ebden). Saving two match points, Zverev and Siegemund won their match in a match tiebreak score of 15–13, and with Kerber's earlier narrow win against [Alja Tomljanovic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajla_Tomljanovi%C4%87), Team Germany qualified for the final against Team [Poland](22936.md).[^cite_note-395] After Kerber was unsuccessful against the Polish world No. 1, [Iga Swiatek](51838558.md), Zverev made a critical comeback win against Poland's [Hubert Hurkacz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hubert_Hurkacz), saving two championship points in the process and levelled the tie 1–1. Partnering once more with Siegemund, Zverev played the mixed doubles match and successfully defeated the Polish pair of Hurkacz and Swiatek in a decisive match tiebreak and won the United Cup for Team Germany.[^cite_note-396] In the process, he won his first team event since the [Laver Cup in 2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Laver_Cup).

[In 2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_United_Cup), Zverev won his opening round-robin matches against [Thiago Monteiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thiago_Monteiro_\(tennis\)) and [Zhang Zhizhen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhang_Zhizhen) but withdrew from his next match due to a bicep strain injury. Team Germany then failed to progress to the quarterfinals, failing to defend their title.[^cite_note-397] [In 2026](81647045.md), Zverev defeated [Tallon Griekspoor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tallon_Griekspoor) in straight sets and won the mixed doubles match alongside [Laura Siegemund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laura_Siegemund) to secure the tie over Netherlands.[^cite_note-398] In their next tie against Poland, Zverev lost in straight sets to [Hubert Hurkacz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hubert_Hurkacz), who was making a return from a seven-month hiatus due to injury.[^cite_note-\:2-329]

## Playing style



Zverev hitting a forehand

Zverev is a baseline counterpuncher who relies on his massive serve and powerful backhand. He is able to hit strong groundstrokes to open the court, and finish points effectively at the net. Although his height slows him down on the court, it also adds to his reach and gives him the ability to get more balls back in play. [Novak Djokovic](16100029.md) has commented, "He moves well for his height."[^cite_note-Djokovic-praise-399] His backhand, in particular, is regarded as his strength and one of the best in the game. He can generate a lot of power off it with high accuracy, and his ability to absorb pace makes it one of the best shots in the modern game for both attack and defense.[^cite_note-economist-11] He often uses a semi-open stance and employs a western grip on his forehand. He has a continental-eastern grip on his backhand. Zverev can also slice his backhands that require a low centre of gravity, despite his height.[^cite_note-400] He is often criticized for being too passive in the big moments.[^cite_note-401]

With Zverev's height of 1.98 metres (6 ft 6 in), he can generate big serves at 220 kilometres per hour (140 mph) or faster at sharper angles than shorter players.[^cite_note-economist-11] The year Zverev first broke into the top 20, he was still not one of the better servers on tour, ranking just 38th in serve rating. This was well behind him ranking 18th in return rating, showing that his return game was much stronger than his serving.[^cite_note-402] By 2018, he had improved in both categories, ranking 19th in serve rating and 8th in return rating. Zverev excels in particular at hitting a high percentage of first serves in, landing 64.2% in 2018. In his return game, he had the fifth highest percentage of first serve points won that year at 32.4%.[^cite_note-2018-serve-rating-403][^cite_note-2018-return-rating-404] His second serve has been a weakness for him in his earlier years, yet he has managed to turn it into a consistent strength. Zverev hit the most double faults on tour in 2016 and 2020.[^cite_note-405] While it has improved since, it can still cause trouble for the German in big points.



Zverev serving

Zverev is an all-court player and does not aim to be better on any surface in particular. He has said, "I feel like I can play on all surfaces. I've been to two finals or won tournaments on every single surface. I don't feel like I have to focus on one... I feel like I have good chances at all of them."[^cite_note-406] [Toni Nadal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toni_Nadal), the uncle and coach of [Rafael Nadal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Nadal), has praised his ability on hard courts in particular, saying, "His best surface is, in my eyes, the hard court, because he moves better than he does on clay."[^cite_note-407] Six of Zverev's first ten titles came on hard courts, while the other four were on clay. As of 2024, he has yet to win a title on grass, but has reached two finals and defeated Federer on that surface. His best grass tournament remains [Halle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halle_Open), in his home country.[^cite_note-itf-profile-27][^cite_note-federer-upset-quote-part-1-52]

Zverev has the ability to [serve-and-volley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serve-and-volley) as well. His older brother Mischa is regarded as the biggest proponent of this playing style on the modern tour.[^cite_note-408] Although Zverev does not employ this technique as often as his brother, he has shown it can be effective in big matches such as the Madrid Masters final against [Dominic Thiem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominic_Thiem), a player who rarely comes to the net.[^cite_note-409] Zverev's volley and overhead technique are regarded as some of his biggest weaknesses.[^cite_note-telegraph-family-interview-13]

Zverev was tall but skinny and not very muscular when growing up.[^cite_note-telegraph-family-interview-13] His fitness trainer Jez Green has focused on making him stronger and set a goal for him to add 4 kilograms (8.8 lb) of muscle each year. Initially, Zverev had difficulty adjusting to this training style on the court, saying, "I had no idea what was going on in my body", and needed to adapt his shot technique as he became more muscular. Eventually, he improved on the court. Green remarked, "He started to become less wobbly. He started to absorb the power, so when someone hit hard he could hit the ball back and everything locked into place."[^cite_note-economist-11]

## Coaching team

Zverev has been coached by his parents since he was very young. His mother was initially his primary coach before his father took over at some point.[^cite_note-economist-11] Zverev made the decision to hire former world No. 1 [Juan Carlos Ferrero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Carlos_Ferrero) at the [Washington Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Citi_Open) in the summer of 2017.[^cite_note-ny-times-rise-410] He fired Ferrero following the [2018 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Australian_Open) after Ferrero criticized the rest of Zverev's coaching team.[^cite_note-411] [Ivan Lendl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivan_Lendl), another former world No. 1, joined Zverev's team in August 2018.[^cite_note-412] They split up in July 2019 due to disappointing results and personal differences. Zverev has stated that Lendl was more interested in his dog or his golf game than in professional coaching.[^cite_note-413] In 2020, Zverev started to work with former World Number 3 and 2013 French Open Finalist [David Ferrer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Ferrer). They announced their split in January 2021.[^cite_note-414] Zverev's coaching team also includes physio Hugo Gravil. Fitness trainer Jez Green, who previously worked with [Andy Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Murray), also worked with Zverev beginning 2013.[^cite_note-economist-11][^cite_note-ny-times-rise-410] Green departed Zverev's team in 2021 after working with him for around seven years.[^cite_note-415]

## Controversies

|  | This ["criticism" or "controversy" section](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Criticism#Avoid_sections_and_articles_focusing_on_criticisms_or_controversies) **may compromise the article's [neutrality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Neutral_point_of_view#Article_structure)**. Please help [integrate negative information](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Structure) into other sections or remove [undue focus on minor criticisms and controversies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:BALASP). *(July 2026)* |

### COVID-19 pandemic

In June 2020, following Novak Djokovic's [Adria Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adria_Tour), several players tested positive for COVID-19. Due to his possible exposure, Zverev announced on Twitter that he would be following self-isolation procedures after testing negative for the virus. However, one week later, Zverev was captured on video at a party in [Monaco](19261.md),[^cite_note-416] prompting criticism from fellow tennis players [Nick Kyrgios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Kyrgios) and [Katie Boulter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katie_Boulter).[^cite_note-417][^cite_note-418][^cite_note-419]

### First allegation of domestic abuse

In October 2020, Zverev's ex-girlfriend Olga Sharypova, in an article in *Racquet* magazine by journalist Ben Rothenberg, accused Zverev of physically and emotionally abusing her over the course of their relationship.[^cite_note-ex_girlfriend_of-420] Sharypova named multiple instances where Zverev allegedly became violent towards her, including punching her in the face during an argument that took place in the pair's hotel room while Zverev was competing at the [2019 Laver Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Laver_Cup).[^cite_note-421] Zverev denied the allegations and issued a statement after Sharypova's initial accusations: "I very much regret that she makes such statements. Because the accusations are simply not true."[^cite_note-ex_girlfriend_of-420] A second article by Rothenberg about the allegations was released in August 2021, this time in *[Slate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slate_\(magazine\))* magazine. The second article picked up Sharypova's story where the first left off. It includes this description of the alleged violence escalating in October 2019:

> &#x20;Sharypova said she went to take a shower, during which Zverev continued berating her from outside the bathroom door. "When I got out of the shower, I was starting to take a towel and he came and said, 'Pack your stuff right now and leave,'" Sharypova recalled. "I'm just like, 'OK, can you wait a few minutes please? I'm naked here.'" From there, Sharypova said, Zverev attacked her more violently than he ever had before. She said that he grabbed her by the throat and pushed her up against the hard tile wall of the bathroom. "He started to punch me, and this time I understand that I can't be dough for punching," she said.[^cite_note-RothenbergSlate-422]

Rothenberg reports that "Sharypova has repeatedly said that she is not interested in pursuing criminal or civil action against Zverev." She told Rothenberg she wanted to be open and honest to help other women who tend to stay silent in such situations due to fear of not being believed.[^cite_note-RothenbergSlate-422] Zverev secured an injunction from a Berlin court against *Slate* later in August 2021, barring it from publishing the assault allegations without stronger evidence. In response, *Slate* stated that it stands by the reporting in the article and has not removed the article from its website. They protested that the injunction was obtained without the organization having an opportunity to present evidence, and they appealed the decision.[^cite_note-Zverev-injunction-2021-423]

Commentator [Mary Carillo](2303019.md) stepped down from her presenting role at the [2021 Laver Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Laver_Cup) in response to the ATP's handling of the allegations.[^cite_note-424][^cite_note-425] Following a 15-month ATP-commissioned investigation conducted by The Lake Forest Group (a third-party consultant working with the ATP's outside legal counsel, Smith Hulsey & Busey), the ATP announced in late January 2023 that they would not punish Zverev due to insufficient evidence to substantiate the allegations.[^cite_note-Zverev-atpinvestigation-426]

### 2022 Mexican Open default

After a first-round [doubles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Abierto_Mexicano_Telcel_%E2%80%93_Doubles) loss at the [2022 Mexican Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Abierto_Mexicano_Telcel), Zverev repeatedly and violently struck umpire Alessandro Germani's chair with his racket, believing that Germani had incorrectly called a ball out during a tiebreak.[^cite_note-427][^cite_note-428] Zverev was subsequently defaulted from the tournament, forfeiting all points and prize money that he had won thus far.[^cite_note-Laughton-166][^cite_note-429][^cite_note-430] He received the maximum fine of \$40,000 by the ATP,[^cite_note-431][^cite_note-432] an additional \$25,000 fine, and a suspended eight-week ban.[^cite_note-433] He was also placed on probation for one year.[^cite_note-434][^cite_note-435] Zverev later issued an apology on social media.[^cite_note-436][^cite_note-437]

### Second allegation of domestic abuse

About six months after the ATP declined to take action against Zverev, in July 2023, allegations from a second woman became public through a court filing. The public prosecution office requested a penalty order against Zverev in a Berlin criminal court over alleged bodily harm against Brenda Patea, Zverev's ex-girlfriend and the mother of his daughter.[^cite_note-438] On 2 October 2023, the court issued a summary penalty order against Zverev, which included a fine of €450,000.[^cite_note-ESPNJan24-439][^cite_note-Guardian2024-440] In Germany, summary orders can be issued, typically without a prior hearing, when a judge considers the evidence sufficient to support the accusation and a trial is not deemed necessary.[^cite_note-ESPNJan24-439] Zverev lodged an objection against the order, which under German law resulted in a public trial.[^cite_note-ESPNJan24-439] Patea spoke publicly for the first time about the alleged abuse on 1 November 2023 in an interview with the *[Süddeutsche Zeitung](30873120.md)*. She said that during a dispute in May 2020, Zverev pushed her into a wall and choked her.[^cite_note-TelegraphNov23-441]

In January 2024, Zverev was appointed to serve on the ATP Players Advisory Council. The ATP has been criticized for allowing him to serve on the council while the domestic abuse charge is pending in a criminal court.[^cite_note-442][^cite_note-443] Also in January 2024, Zverev was featured on the second season of the [Netflix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netflix) series *[Break Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Break_Point_\(2023_TV_series\))* in an episode focused on his comeback from injury. Netflix was criticized for ignoring both allegations of domestic abuse. One columnist argued that "*Break Point* promised to take viewers behind the curtain of professional tennis and reveal the personalities behind it. That promise is null and void if Zverev – and the media – isn't willing to be honest."[^cite_note-\:0-444]

At the beginning of the [2024 Australian Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Australian_Open), a court official announced that Zverev was scheduled to stand trial before the Tiergarten District Court in [Berlin](3354.md).[^cite_note-CNN2024-445][^cite_note-446] The timing of the announcement, combined with Zverev's semifinal run at the tournament, brought heightened interest in and scrutiny of the allegations from both the press and the public.[^cite_note-447][^cite_note-448][^cite_note-449] The trial began on 31 May 2024.[^cite_note-450] On 7 June 2024, the third day of the trial, the judge agreed to a request by the public prosecution office, endorsed by the alleged victim, to provisionally terminate the proceedings subject to the condition that Zverev pay €200,000, of which €150,000 would go to the German State Treasury and €50,000 to charitable organizations.[^cite_note-\:4-451][^cite_note-\:5-452] The trial therefore concluded without a finding of guilt.[^cite_note-\:5-452] The prosecutor's request to terminate the proceedings followed an out-of-court agreement between Zverev and the alleged victim, the terms of which were not disclosed.[^cite_note-\:4-451] According to his counsel, Zverev entered into the agreement "to put a quick end to the proceedings – above all in the interest of their child", emphasizing that the agreement "includes no determination or admission of guilt".[^cite_note-453]

## Personal life

Zverev lives in [Monte Carlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monte_Carlo), Monaco.[^cite_note-ny-times-rise-410] He grew up in Hamburg and also has spent his winters living in Florida at the [Saddlebrook Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saddlebrook_Academies).[^cite_note-itf-junior-interview-12][^cite_note-economist-11] He can speak German, Russian, and English.[^cite_note-atp-bio-454] Zverev has a daughter, Mayla, who was born in 2021 to Brenda Patea, an ex-girlfriend.[^cite_note-455][^cite_note-456][^cite_note-457] Both parents reached an out-of-court financial settlement after a lawsuit over alleged physical assault was filed against Zverev by the ex-partner in 2023.[^cite_note-458] Since 2021, Zverev has been dating German actress [Sophia Thomalla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sophia_Thomalla).[^cite_note-459]

In 2017, Zverev said that his tennis idol is [Roger Federer](262376.md).[^cite_note-telegraph-family-interview-13] He is a fan of the [Miami Heat](72850.md)[^cite_note-itf-junior-interview-12] and [Bayern Munich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FC_Bayern_Munich) and wore the jersey of Bayern player [Michael Olise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Olise) during the [2026 BMW Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_BMW_Open).[^cite_note-460] In his spare time, he enjoys playing basketball and golf. His best friend on the tennis tour is Brazilian doubles specialist [Marcelo Melo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcelo_Melo).[^cite_note-461][^cite_note-462][^cite_note-best-friend-463] He is also close friends with [Dominic Thiem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominic_Thiem) and [Andrey Rublev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrey_Rublev), and has known the latter since childhood.[^cite_note-464][^cite_note-465] Zverev filed a lawsuit against the ACE Group International and its CEO, [Patricio Apey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patricio_Apey), accusing them of "unlawful restraint of trade". He argued that the contract between them was oppressive, but eventually agreed to settle the dispute out of court before the trial began.[^cite_note-466][^cite_note-467]

### Health

In August 2022, Zverev announced that he has [Type 1 diabetes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Type_1_diabetes), having been diagnosed at the age of three.[^cite_note-468][^cite_note-469] That year, he launched the Alexander Zverev Foundation, a charity to support people with diabetes.[^cite_note-470] During a match at the [2023 French Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_French_Open), Zverev was not permitted to inject [insulin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insulin) on-court,[^cite_note-471] prompting criticism from the [International Diabetes Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Diabetes_Federation)[^cite_note-472] and the [JDRF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JDRF).[^cite_note-473] Tournament organizers later clarified that Zverev would be allowed to inject insulin on-court.[^cite_note-474]
